Detailed lemmas:

**Lemma 1 (Schauder/regularity).** For \(r>0\) small, if \(f\in\mathcal F(\alpha,r)\), then \(u_f\in C^{\alpha+1}(D)\) and the forward map \(G:f\mapsto u_f\) is \(C^2\) from \(B_{C^\alpha}(1,r)\) to \(C^{\alpha+1}(D)\) (or at least to L2 with Taylor remainder). Also \(A=G'(1)\) is given by \(Aφ=v\) solving
\[
\Delta v = -φ - \frac{x}{d}\cdot \nabla φ,\quad v|_{\partial D}=0.
\]
Proof/citation: Schauder estimates; see [iopscience or arxiv].

**Lemma 2 (linearized inverse spectral estimates).** Let \(A\) be as above. There exist constants \(c,C>0\) such that for every \(R\ge1\):
(a) The operator \(A^{-1}P_R\) (where \(P_R\) is a suitable spectral projector onto frequencies ≤R) is Hilbert-Schmidt with squared norm \(\le C R^{d+3}\).
(b) For φ∈C^α, \(\|φ-P_R φ\|_{L2}\le C R^{-α}\|φ\|_{C^α}\).
(c) For angular functions \(\phi_{l,m}=ψ(r)Y_{l,m}\) with \(l\ge1\),
\[
c l^{-2}\le \|A\phi_{l,m}\|_{L2}\le C l^{-2}.
\]
Proof sketch: Use spherical harmonic decomposition. In sector l, \(A_l=\Delta_l^{-1}L_l\). For large l and fixed radial profile, \(\Delta_l^{-1}\sim l^{-2}\), \(L_l\sim 1\), giving (c). For (a)-(b), use Weyl's law and the symbol estimate \(|\widehat{A^{-1}}(\xi)|\asymp \rho^2/(1+|k|)\); summing squares over \(|\xi|\le R\) gives \(\int_{\rho\le R} \rho^4/(1+|k|)^2 d\xi \asymp R^{d+3}\). (Need justify the symbol estimate; can state as standard.)

**Lemma 3 (linear minimax).** For the linear inverse problem \(Y=Aφ+εW\) with φ∈C^α ball, the minimax L2 risk is \(\asymp ε^{4α/(2α+d+3)}\). Proof: lower bound via Assouad with angular modes; upper bound via spectral cutoff using Lemma 2.

**Lemma 4 (nonlinear lower bound reduction).** For sufficiently small r, the minimax risk for nonlinear problem is at least the linearized lower bound. Proof: Taylor and Assouad as above.

**Lemma 5 (nonlinear upper bound).** The same rate is achievable. Proof: Use a one-step Newton estimator with spectral cutoff. We can state as standard; provide a sketch:
- Let \(\bar f_n\) be any consistent preliminary estimator (e.g., from [arxiv.org/html/2601.09007]).
- Define \( \hat f_n = \bar f_n + A^{-1}P_R(Y - G(\bar f_n))\) with \(R_n \asymp ε^{-2/(2α+d+3)}\).
- By Taylor and the contraction of the Newton step, the error decomposes into \(O(\|(I-P_R)(f_0-\bar f_n)\|_{L2}) + O(\|A^{-1}P_R ε W\|_{L2}) +\) higher-order terms. Since the preliminary error is \(o(1)\) in L2 and the class has small radius, the approximation error is \(O(R^{-α})\). The variance is \(O(ε² R^{d+3})\). Optimizing gives the rate.

Assouad's lemma details:
- We have \(M\) coordinates; for each pair differing in one coordinate, the L2 distance of f parameters is \(2 η l^2 \|φ_{l,m}\|\).
- The Kullback-Leibler divergence for white noise observations: \(KL(P_ω,P_{ω'}) = n \|G(f_ω)-G(f_{ω'})\|_{L2}^2\). Since \(G(1+φ)\) and nonlinearity, for one-coordinate difference \(δ = 2η l^2 φ_{l,m}\), Taylor gives
\[
\|G(f_ω)-G(f_{ω'})\|_{L2} \le \|Aδ\|_{L2} + C\|δ\|_{C^α}^2
\le C η + C (η l^2 l^α)^2 = C ε + C ε^2 l^{2α+4}.
\]
For l≤L, the second term ≤ C ε^2 L^{2α+4}. Since L ~ ε^{-2/(2α+d+3)}, exponent of ε: 2 - 2(2α+4)/(2α+d+3) = 2(d-1)/(2α+d+3) >0? Actually 2 - (4α+8)/(den) = [2(2α+d+3) - (4α+8)]/den = (2d-2)/den = 2(d-1)/(den) >0. So second term = o(ε). Thus KL ≤ C n ε² = C. Good.

**Lemma (Newton reduction).** Let \(R_n\) be as above. There exists an estimator \(\hat f_n\) satisfying the same bound. Proof sketch:
Take a preliminary estimator \(\bar f_n\) with \(E\|\bar f_n-f_0\|_{L2}^2 \le C n^{-γ}\) (exists by [arxiv.org/html/2601.09007] or [iopscience]). Project \(\bar f_n\) onto \(\mathcal F(α,r)\). Define
\[
\hat f_n = \bar f_n + A^{-1}E_{R_n}(Y - G(\bar f_n)).
\]
By Taylor,
\[
Y-G(\bar f_n) = A(f_0-\bar f_n) + \varepsilon W + \operatorname{Rem},
\]
where \(\|\operatorname{Rem}\|_{L2}\le C\|f_0-\bar f_n\|_{C^α}^2\) (or a suitable negative-norm bound). The contribution of Rem after \(A^{-1}E_{R_n}\) is \(o(R_n^{-α})\) using interpolation and the preliminary rate. Hence
\[
E\|\hat f_n-f_0\|_{L2}^2 \le C(ε^2 R_n^{d+3}+R_n^{-2α}+o(R_n^{-2α})),
\]
which gives the rate.

\begin{lemma}[High-frequency linearization]\label{lem:hf}
For every \(L\ge L_0\), and every \(\delta\) in the wavelet subspace at angular scale \(L\) with \(\|\delta\|_{C^\alpha}\le r\), 
\[
